    my witness is not true; another is the one who bears witness about me."
  - John 20:22–23 (the commissioning — Spirit received, then the remit /
    retain saying): καὶ τοῦτο εἰπὼν ἐνεφύσησεν καὶ λέγει αὐτοῖς· λάβετε
    πνεῦμα ἅγιον· ἄν τινων ἀφῆτε τὰς ἁμαρτίας ἀφέωνται αὐτοῖς, ἄν τινων
    κρατῆτε κεκράτηνται — the sending saying of 20:21 is immediately followed
    by "receive the Holy Spirit" (λάβετε, imperative) and then the saying:
    "if you forgive anyone's sins, they are forgiven them; if you retain
    anyone's, they are retained." The sequence in the text is sending, then
    Spirit received, then the saying — in that order.
  - John 17:20–23 (the oneness — prayed for those beyond the first disciples):
    Οὐ περὶ τούτων δὲ ἐρωτῶ μόνον, ἀλλὰ καὶ περὶ τῶν πιστευόντων διὰ τοῦ
    λόγου αὐτῶν εἰς ἐμέ, ἵνα πάντες ἓν ὦσιν, καθὼς σύ, πάτερ, ἐν ἐμοὶ κἀγὼ
    ἐν σοί, ἵνα καὶ αὐτοὶ ἐν ἡμῖν ὦσιν — the prayer explicitly extends beyond
    the first disciples to those who believe through their word, that all may
    be one "as you, Father, are in me and I in you, that they also may be in
    us"; ἐγὼ ἐν αὐτοῖς καὶ σὺ ἐν ἐμοί, ἵνα ὦσιν τετελειωμένοι εἰς ἕν (17:23).
    What is prayed for the wider company is oneness and indwelling — ἐγὼ ἐν
    αὐτοῖς καὶ σὺ ἐν ἐμοί — with its stated purpose, quoted in full: ἵνα
    γινώσκῃ ὁ κόσμος ὅτι σύ με ἀπέστειλας καὶ ἠγάπησας αὐτοὺς καθὼς ἐμὲ
    ἠγάπησας (17:23) — "that the world may know that you sent me *and loved
    them as (καθὼς) you loved me*." The ὅτι clause has two halves, and the
    second carries a further καθὼς: the Father's love for them is patterned
    on his love for the Son, stated in the same breath as his sending of the
    Son. (An earlier draft of this link quoted only the first half of the
    clause; the full clause is a chain receipt, not a trimming.)
- **The close (settled):** Within the supplied Johannine set, two parallels are
  stated by the text itself, not assembled by the reader. (1) The sending
  parallel: the disciples are sent καθὼς the Son was sent (17:18; 20:21).
  (2) The non-self-origination parallel: the Son can do οὐδέν ἀφʼ ἑαυτοῦ,
  acting as he sees (5:19), hears (5:30), and is commanded (12:49–50), with
  the Father abiding in him doing the works (14:10); the disciples can do
  οὐδέν χωρὶς ἐμοῦ, bearing fruit only as branches abiding in the vine
  (15:4–5), asking in his name while he does it (14:13–14), witnessing
  alongside the sent Paraclete (15:26–27), and receiving the Spirit in the
  same breath as their sending (20:21–22). A third stated parallel supports
  the second without being a sending text: the one who eats the Son abides
  in him and he in that one (6:56), and will live διʼ ἐμέ as the Son lives
  διὰ τὸν πατέρα (6:57) — life itself is lived through another, at both
  steps of the chain. The reception runs the same direction: trust, sight,
  and honor directed at the Son are directed at the sender (12:44–45; 14:9;
  5:23), and judgment of the rejecter rests not in the Son's independent
  initiative but in the word he spoke (12:47–48) — the word given him by
  commandment (12:49). No supplied verse presents a sent
  one — Son or disciples — acting from himself. The old entry's denial of
  independent authority therefore stands, now with receipts; its wording does
  not (see the proposed close).
- **The proposed close (marked):** The old entry's "the Son's relationship
  with the Father transferred to us" should be replaced. No supplied verse
  says a relationship was transferred: 17:22 says the glory given to the Son
  was given to them (τὴν δόξαν ἣν δέδωκάς μοι δέδωκα αὐτοῖς) so that they may
  be one, and 17:21, 23 speak of being *in* — αὐτοὶ ἐν ἡμῖν, ἐγὼ ἐν αὐτοῖς
  καὶ σὺ ἐν ἐμοί — not of a relationship handed over. The proposed wording,
  as the proposer's summary of the chain: the household is commissioned *on
  the Son's pattern and in participation in his indwelling oneness with the
  Father*, not by transfer of his relationship. The old entry had a second
  sentence, and it is replaced too, explicitly, so nothing of it is silently
  retained: its denial — "we are not granted independent authority" — stands
  (settled close), but its general formula — "God by His Spirit is actively
  at work within us as we live in continuous union with Him" — is replaced
  by the set's specifics, as the proposer's summary: *its members do not act
  from themselves, but bear fruit abiding in the Son (15:4–5), live through
  him as he lives through the Father (6:57), ask in his name while he does
  what is asked (14:12–14), and witness alongside the Paraclete, having
  received the Spirit at their sending (15:26–27; 20:21–22).* One scope
  note, marked as proposed: the sending verbs of 17:18 and 20:21 address the
  first disciples; 17:20–23 extends the oneness and indwelling prayer to
  later believers explicitly, but does not restate the sending for them —
  applying the commission itself to the whole household is the proposer's
  extension of the pattern, not a settled datum of these verses. The scope
  note governs the *sending* only: the *mode* statements in the set are
  already framed universally, in singular participial constructions that
  name whoever stands in the relation — ὁ πιστεύων (5:24; 12:44; 14:12),
  ὁ τρώγων (6:54, 56), ὁ μένων / ἐὰν μή τις μένῃ (15:5–6). Extending the
  public sending beyond the first disciples is proposed; the non-self-
  originating mode is not an extension — the set states it for the believer,
  the eater, and the one abiding, as such.
- **Honesty clause:** (1) The entry's title word is ἀποστέλλω, but the supplied
  set carries the sending pattern on both ἀποστέλλω (6:57; 17:18, 21, 23;
  20:21) and πέμπω (5:23, 24, 30; 12:44, 45, 49; 15:26; 20:21, second
  clause). No
  claim in this entry rests on ἀποστέλλω alone, and none may be quoted as if
  it did. (2) No supplied verse uses "household" or "commissioned household";
  both are the KB's category labels, retained from the old entry's title.
  (3) No supplied verse states the general formula "God by His Spirit is
  actively at work within us." What the set states is specific: the Father
  abiding in the *Son* does his works (14:10); the disciples receive the
  Spirit at their sending (20:22); the Paraclete will witness and they also
  will witness (15:26–27); the Son is in them and the Father in the Son
  (17:23). The old entry's formula assembles those into a general statement —
  the assembly is the proposer's, and the entry now rests on the specifics.
  (4) John 20:23 is not explained away: the remit / retain saying states a
  real effect in the perfect tense (ἀφέωνται, κεκράτηνται). This entry builds
  nothing on its mechanics and denies none of it; it observes only that the
  saying stands after λάβετε πνεῦμα ἅγιον in the text's own sequence, inside
  the καθὼς sending of 20:21. (5) John 14:12's "greater works" (μείζονα
  τούτων) is quoted, not shrunk: the verse promises them and gives its own
  ground — ὅτι ἐγὼ πρὸς τὸν πατέρα πορεύομαι — and 14:13–14 assigns the
  doing to the Son in answer to asking in his name. This entry does not
  adjudicate what the greater works are. (6) John 5:21–27 gives the Son
  life-giving and judgment functions the set nowhere gives the disciples
  (οὕτως καὶ ὁ υἱὸς οὓς θέλει ζῳοποιεῖ, 5:21; τὴν κρίσιν πᾶσαν δέδωκεν τῷ
  υἱῷ, 5:22). The καθὼς parallels of 17:18 and 20:21 are parallels of
  sending and of mode, not an equation of the sent ones' roles.
  (7) John 6:54–58 is used only for what its verses state: the eater has
  eternal life and will be raised at the last day (6:54), abides in the Son
  and the Son in him (6:56), and will live through him (6:57), as the one who
  eats this bread will live forever (6:58). This entry builds nothing on what
  eating the flesh and drinking the blood means beyond those statements, and
  nothing on 6:57 as a sending text — its sending verb belongs to the
  Father–Son side of the analogy only.
- **Rival chain (stated fairly):** (a) The delegated-independence reading:
  20:23 grants the disciples authority to remit and retain, and 14:12
  promises they will do greater works — the commission is a real grant of
  authority to act in his place. Answered: the grant-shaped texts are real
  and are quoted above, not denied; but neither text presents the acting as
  self-originated. 14:13–14 immediately assigns the doing of what is asked
  in his name to the Son himself (τοῦτο ποιήσω ... ἐγὼ ποιήσω), and 20:23
  follows the imperative λάβετε πνεῦμα ἅγιον (20:22) and the καθὼς sending
  (20:21). Authority exercised inside abiding, asking, and Spirit-reception
  is what the set shows; independence is the one mode every inability verse
  in the set denies (5:19, 30; 12:49; 14:10; 15:5). (b) The no-parallel
  reading: the Son's sending is unique and the disciples are merely tasked
  messengers, so nothing about his mode carries over. Answered: John himself
  draws the parallel twice with καθὼς (17:18; 20:21) and repeats the Son's
  inability construction for the disciples (15:4–5 against 5:19) — the
  parallel is the text's, though Honesty clause (6) marks where it stops.
  (c) The transfer reading (the old entry's own wording): 17:22's given glory
  and 17:23's indwelling amount to the Son's relationship being transferred
  to the disciples. Answered: the verbs are giving (δέδωκα) of glory for a
  stated purpose (ἵνα ὦσιν ἕν) and being-in (ἐγὼ ἐν αὐτοῖς καὶ σὺ ἐν ἐμοί);
  no supplied verse uses a transfer verb of the relationship itself.
  What is given is glory (17:22); what is prayed is indwelling and oneness
  (17:21, 23) — neither is stated as a relationship handed over.
